===== evidence/ellis-assessment/04-ellis-assignment-authority-conclusion.txt =====

# Ellis Assignment Authority Conclusion

Assessment Scope:

Determine whether Ellis Assignment Authority exists only as governance meaning or as a specified architectural subsystem.

Evidence Reviewed:

- docs/governance/CAPABILITY_ROUTING_MODEL.md

- docs/governance/CANONICAL_ENVELOPE_SPECIFICATION.md

- docs/governance/GOVERNANCE_LIFECYCLE_STATE_MODEL.md

- docs/governance/GOVERNANCE_VALIDATION_SPECIFICATION.md

- docs/governance/HEADQUARTERS_ORGANIZATIONAL_CHARTER.md

- db/governance-lifecycle-enforcement.ts

Findings:

- Ellis is a canonical governance authority.

- Ellis owns assignment.

- Ellis owns capability resolution.

- Ellis determines ownership.

- Ellis determines routing state.

- Assignment is a canonical lifecycle stage.

- The ASSIGNED lifecycle state is defined as Ellis resolving capabilities and assigning ownership.

- Canonical Envelope assignment fields exist:

  - assignment_state

  - assigned_department

  - assigned_actor

  - routing_history

- Mutation authority for assignment fields belongs exclusively to Ellis.

- Governance Validation is explicitly prohibited from performing assignment.

- Operational departments do not own assignment authority.

Runtime Assessment:

Implemented:

- Lifecycle eligibility enforcement.

- Envelope creation eligibility enforcement.

Not Found:

- Ellis runtime implementation.

- Assignment Authority runtime implementation.

- assignment_state persistence.

- assigned_department persistence.

- assigned_actor persistence.

- routing_history persistence.

Conclusion:

Repository evidence supports Ellis Assignment Authority as a specified architectural subsystem with a defined authority model and canonical state model.

Repository evidence does not support an existing Ellis runtime implementation.

Final Classification:

Ellis Assignment Authority: SPECIFIED

Ellis Runtime: NOT IMPLEMENTED

Assignment Authority Runtime Corridor:

Potentially justified by repository evidence.

Implementation remains unauthorized pending separate scope and prioritization decisions.


===== evidence/ellis-assessment/05-assignment-authority-implementation-readiness.txt =====

# Assignment Authority Implementation Readiness Finding

Assessment Scope:

Determine whether repository evidence supports opening an Assignment Authority implementation-readiness corridor.

Evidence Reviewed:

- evidence/ellis-assessment/04-ellis-assignment-authority-conclusion.txt

- db/governance.schema.ts

- db/governance-runtime.ts

- db/governance-lifecycle-enforcement.ts

- scripts/smoke-governance-envelope-runtime.mjs

- scripts/validate-governance-persistence.sh

- server/orchestration/router.ts

- server/orchestration/scheduler.ts

- docs/governance/CANONICAL_ENVELOPE_SPECIFICATION.md

- docs/governance/GOVERNANCE_LIFECYCLE_STATE_MODEL.md

Findings:

- governance_envelopes currently persists Envelope creation state.

- governance_envelopes currently includes Governance Validation-owned Envelope fields:

  - validation_status

  - required_capabilities

  - operational_corridor

- governance_envelopes currently includes lifecycle_state.

- governance_envelopes does not include Ellis-owned assignment fields:

  - assignment_state

  - assigned_department

  - assigned_actor

  - routing_history

- db/governance-runtime.ts supports createGovernanceEnvelope(...).

- db/governance-runtime.ts does not support Ellis assignment mutation.

- Current smoke and persistence validation scripts validate Envelope creation, not assignment persistence.

- server/orchestration/router.ts is a pure task routing primitive.

- server/orchestration/scheduler.ts is a task scheduling primitive.

- Existing task routing/scheduling surfaces are not canonical Envelope assignment authority.

- GOVERNANCE_LIFECYCLE_STATE_MODEL defines ENVELOPE_CREATED -> ASSIGNED as a formal lifecycle transition.

- ASSIGNED means Ellis has resolved capabilities and assigned ownership.

- CANONICAL_ENVELOPE_SPECIFICATION defines assignment_state, assigned_department, assigned_actor, and routing_history as Ellis-owned fields with Ellis-only mutation authority.

Readiness Interpretation:

Repository evidence supports opening an Assignment Authority implementation-readiness corridor.

The smallest plausible implementation surface would be the governance Envelope persistence/runtime layer, not the task router, task scheduler, worker claim system, or execution runtime.

Implementation should remain limited to canonical Ellis assignment state and lifecycle transition readiness unless separately authorized.

Implementation remains unauthorized.

Out Of Scope Unless Separately Authorized:

- Agent invocation.

- Automation.

- Execution Authority.

- Routing Authority beyond assignment-state derivation.

- Task scheduler changes.

- Worker claim changes.

- Generalized lifecycle engine.

===== evidence/ellis-assessment/09-ellis-runtime-shape-reconciliation.txt =====

ELLIS RUNTIME SHAPE RECONCILIATION

Finding:

Ellis is architecturally justified as Assignment/Routing Authority, but current repository evidence does not justify implementing Ellis as a full autonomous or conversational agent.

Repository evidence shows Ellis-shaped runtime primitives already exist:

- server/orchestration/router.ts selects assignedAgent.

- server/orchestration/scheduler.ts gates eligibility using assignedAgent.

- server/orchestration/task-state-machine.ts contains task.route with agent.

- server/orchestration tests validate these primitives.

However, these primitives are not currently wired into a live orchestration runtime as an Ellis subsystem.

Conclusion:

Ellis should be treated as the authority boundary over assignment, routing, ownership determination, and assignment-state mutation.

Recommended future sequence:

1. Name and preserve the Ellis authority boundary.

2. Reconcile router/scheduler/task-state primitives under that boundary.

3. Implement Ellis as a service only when live runtime wiring requires it.

4. Implement Ellis as an agent only if future evidence shows autonomous interpretation, negotiation, or multi-agent coordination cannot be safely handled by a pure service boundary.

Current implementation recommendation:

Do not implement Ellis as an agent now.

Do not create assignment persistence now unless a live runtime consumer requires persisted assignment state.

Preserve current orchestration primitives as evidence of future Ellis runtime shape.


===== evidence/ellis-assessment/10-ellis-capability-resolution-boundary.txt =====

ELLIS CAPABILITY RESOLUTION BOUNDARY

Finding:

Ellis is not equivalent to the existing router, scheduler, task-state machine, or worker claim lane.

Repository evidence shows Governance Validation and Envelope persistence already support the upstream inputs Ellis would consume:

- required_capabilities

- operational_corridor

Repository evidence does not show implemented Ellis-owned assignment outputs:

- assignment_state

- assigned_department

- assigned_actor

- routing_history

The existing orchestration primitives perform lower-level mechanics:

- router.ts selects an assignedAgent from supplied candidates.

- scheduler.ts gates eligibility using assignedAgent.

- task-state-machine.ts permits task.route with an agent.

- worker claim/lease/reclaim logic owns execution-time worker claims.

These primitives do not implement canonical Ellis capability resolution because they do not transform required capabilities into departmental ownership, actor assignment, assignment state, or routing history.

Conclusion:

Ellis remains a specified architectural authority, not an implemented runtime.

The existing router/scheduler/task-state/worker mechanisms should be treated as downstream mechanics that may eventually consume Ellis outputs, not as Ellis itself.

Current implementation recommendation:

Do not implement Ellis as an autonomous agent now.

Do not treat worker claim ownership as Ellis assignment ownership.

Do not treat router assignedAgent selection as canonical Ellis assignment authority.

Do not add Ellis-owned assignment persistence until a live runtime consumer requires assignment_state, assigned_department, assigned_actor, or routing_history.
